=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are N types of products, each having 10^{100} units in stock.
You can buy any non-negative number of units of each product. To buy k units of the i-th product, it costs k^2 P_i yen.
If your total purchase cost is at most M yen, what is the maximum number of units you can buy in total?

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
P_1 \ldots P_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^{5}
- 1 \leq M \leq 10^{18}
- 1 \leq P_i \leq 2 \times 10^{9}
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 9
4 1 9

Sample Output 1

3

If you buy one unit of the 1st product and two units of the 2nd product, the total purchase cost is 1^2 \times 4 + 2^2 \times 1 = 8. It is impossible to buy four or more units in total with a total cost of at most 9 yen, so the answer is 3.

Sample Input 2

10 1000
2 15 6 5 12 1 7 9 17 2

Sample Output 2

53

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Need produce final code with fast input. Use sys.stdin.buffer.read().split().

Implementation details:
import sys
def main():
    data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N=data[0]; M=data[1]; P=data[2:]
    # maybe sort? Not necessary.
    def cost_le(T):
        s=0
        # local variables for speed
        M_local=M
        for p in P:
            c = (T + p) // (2 * p)
            s += p * c * c
            if s > M_local:
                return M_local + 1
        return s
    lo=0; hi=10**18
    while lo < hi:
        mid = (lo + hi + 1) // 2
        if cost_le(mid) <= M:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid - 1
    T=lo
    s=0; total=0
    for p in P:
        c = (T + p) // (2 * p)
        s += p * c * c
        total += c
    ans = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

P = data[2:]
D = [2*p for p in P]
def cost_le(T):
    s=0
    for p, dp in zip(P, D):
        c = (T + p) // dp
        s += p * c * c
        if s > M:
            return M + 1
    return s
But zip overhead maybe. Use index loop? For Python, for p, dp in zip is okay. But 12e6 zip iterations okay. Could use local variables:

Now, let's think about if there is a more subtle issue: The cost function for buying k units is k^2 P_i. The marginal cost of the k-th unit is P_i(k^2 - (k-1)^2) = P_i(2k-1). We used that. Good.

Now, let's consider if the optimal set of units is indeed the cheapest marginal costs. Since each product's marginal costs are increasing, the set of first k_i units for each product is a valid "downward closed" set. The problem of maximizing count under budget with convex costs is equivalent to selecting marginal costs with precedence constraints (must select earlier units before later). Because marginal costs increase, the globally cheapest marginal costs automatically respect precedence: if the k-th marginal of a product is selected, all previous are cheaper, so they would be selected earlier in sorted order. Thus the sorted list of all marginal costs is a valid sequence of purchases. Greedy picking cheapest marginal costs yields optimal. This is standard. Good.

def cost_le(T, P=P, twop=twop, M=M):
    s=0
    for p, tp in zip(P, twop):
        c = (T + p) // tp
        s += p * c * c
        if s > M:
            return M + 1
    return s

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "cost_le" using `for p, tp in zip(P, twop):` and P sorted; if P is a list, zip creates iterator each call. Fine.

Potential micro-optimization: Use index loop:
for i in range(N):
    p = P[i]; tp = twop[i]
...
But zip is okay. For 12 million iterations, zip overhead minimal. Could use local `P_local = P; twop_local = twop` in default args. We'll do:
def cost_le(T, P=P, twop=twop, M=M):
    s = 0
    for p, tp in zip(P, twop):
        c = (T + p) // tp
        s += p * c * c
        if s > M:
            return M + 1
    return s
This is fine.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total" in final maybe includes all units with marginal <=T, but if T is in a gap, total same as at previous marginal. Good.

if s + p*c*c > M: return M+1

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "S(T)" monotonic but not strictly; binary search condition true for all T up to max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total" in final maybe includes all units with marginal <=T, but if T is in a gap, total same as at previous marginal. Good.

We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total" in final maybe includes all units with marginal <=T, but if T is in a gap, total same as at previous marginal. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "P_i" values and "M" values all integers, but "T" can be 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost_le(mid)" for mid values where c is large and s overflows? Python no. But if we wanted to cap earlier to avoid huge multiplication, we could check if p*c*c > M - s before adding? Actually to avoid big ints, we can do:
if s + p*c*c > M: return M+1

if s + p*c*c > M: return M+1

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "S(T)" monotonic but not strictly; binary search condition true for all T up to max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "S(T)" monotonic but not strictly; binary search condition true for all T up to max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total" in final maybe includes all units with marginal <=T, but if T is in a gap, total same as at previous marginal. Good.

if s + p*c*c > M: return M+1

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "S(T)" monotonic but not strictly; binary search condition true for all T up to max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total" in final maybe includes all units with marginal <=T, but if T is in a gap, total same as at previous marginal. Good.

We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total" in final maybe includes all units with marginal <=T, but if T is in a gap, total same as at previous marginal. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "P_i" values and "M" values all integers, but "T" can be 0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "cost_le(mid)" for mid values where c is large and s overflows? Python no. But if we wanted to cap earlier to avoid huge multiplication, we could check if p*c*c > M - s before adding? Actually to avoid big ints, we can do:
if s + p*c*c > M: return M+1

if s + p*c*c > M: return M+1

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "S(T)" monotonic but not strictly; binary search condition true for all T up to max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "S(T)" monotonic but not strictly; binary search condition true for all T up to max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "total" in final maybe includes all units with marginal <=T, but if T is in a gap, total same as at previous marginal. Good.

if s + p*c*c > M: return M+1

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "answer = total + (M - s) // (T + 1)" if T+1 is not the next marginal because S(T+1)>M but there could be a marginal cost less than T+1 but greater than T? No integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "T" being max integer with S(T)<=M, but if S(T)<=M for all T up to hi? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "M - s" maybe large and q could be >0; if q >0, we add q units. But do we need to ensure q units are available in the next block? We argued q < D because S(T+1)>M. Let's prove: D = C(T+1)-C(T). S(T+1)=S(T)+D*(T+1). Since S(T+1)>M, D*(T+1) > M-S(T). Thus q = floor((M-S)/ (T+1)) < D. So q <= D-1. Thus we don't exceed block. Good. If S(T+1)=M, then T wouldn't be max (T+1 also true). So strict. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
I need to solve the problem. Let's reason carefully.

We have N products, each with huge stock. Buying k units of product i costs P_i * k^2. Need maximize total units with total cost <= M.

This is equivalent to selecting units with marginal costs. The k-th unit of product i has marginal cost P_i * (2k - 1). We can buy units in increasing marginal cost order. Need count how many of the cheapest marginal costs fit in budget M.

For a threshold T, let c_i(T) = number of units of product i with marginal cost <= T. Then c_i = (T + P_i) // (2 * P_i). The total cost to buy all those units is sum P_i * c_i^2. Let S(T) be that cost, C(T) be total count.

We can binary search the largest T such that S(T) <= M. Then all C(T) units with marginal cost <= T are affordable. The next marginal costs are all T+1 (since S(T+1) > M). We can buy as many of those as remaining budget allows: (M - S(T)) // (T+1). Answer = C(T) + that.

Need ensure hi large enough. M <= 1e18, P_i <= 2e9. 10^18 is safely above any relevant marginal cost, and S(10^18) > M. Use hi=10**18.

Implementation with early exit when cost exceeds M. Sorting P ascending helps early exit.

Let's write final code.</think>```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    M = data[1]
    P = data[2:]

    P.sort()
    twice = [2 * p for p in P]

    def cost_le(T):
        s = 0
        for p, tp in zip(P, twice):
            c = (T + p) // tp
            s += p * c * c
            if s > M:
                return M + 1
        return s

    lo = 0
    hi = 10**18
    while lo < hi:
        mid = (lo + hi + 1) // 2
        if cost_le(mid) <= M:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid - 1

    T = lo
    total_cost = 0
    total_cnt = 0

    for p, tp in zip(P, twice):
        c = (T + p) // tp
        total_cost += p * c * c
        total_cnt += c

    ans = total_cnt + (M - total_cost) // (T + 1)
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
